AUTHORISATION FOR THE RELEASE OF INFORMATION

Applicant:

Case Number: 20-09-2005-091135-004 Expiry: 17 October 2005

20-09-2005-090445-002 17 October 2005
19-09-2005-175937-018 17 October 2005
19-09-2005-175447-017 17 October 2005

The Applicant has made the following request for information:
This applicant made 27 individual FOI requests on the same day about UFO

incidents spanning 1950 to 1980. The MOD files for the period covered by 23
of these are already open for viewing at The National Archives and the
applicant has been advised. This response covers the 4 remaining requests
for which the files are still held by the MOD.

| am researching police officer sightings that have been reported to the MOD
and any subsequent investigations into them. | request all information /
documents with regard to the following sightings;

1). 26 January 1979 at 2045. Location- Denbigh, North Wales. Two uniformed
officers, PC Berwyn Jones and PC Neville Hughes observed a UFO at low
altitude along with several members of the public over Denbigh.

2). Late 1979 - Dumfries, Scotland. Five uniformed officers including PS Bill
McDavid and PC James Smith observed a UFO over the above location.
Seen by several members of the public. Reported in the press.

3). 29 November 1980 at 0400. Location-Moors Keighley / Todmorden area,
West Yorkshire. Four uniformed officers including PC John Porter, PC
Howard Turnpenny and PC Julie Baxter observed blue UFO over valley in the
above area. Officially reported to WYP HQ at Wakefield.

4). 29 November 1980 at 0500-0550. Location-Todmorden, West Yorkshire.
Famous case involving PC Alan Godfrey who sees detailed UFO above the
road in Todmorden. Experiences missing time. Later videotaped under
hypnosis revealing alleged alien abduction experience. Officially reported.
Also seen by a member of the public minutes prior to PC Godfrey's sighting.

Case for release of information

A search of all the known surviving UFO report and correspondence files for
1979 and 1980 has revealed the following documents relevant to these
requests.

1), UFO report made by PC Hughes and PC Berwyn Jones on 26 January
1979. A member of the public also made a report about the same incident
and this was received by the MOD from two sources. Copies of these are
attached. The name and address of the member of the public has been
removed as it is not relevant to the request.
2). There were no UFO reports matching the details given by the applicant in
“late 1979” but there was one made on 30 August 1980. A copy is attached.
The personal details of a member of the public has been removed because it
is not relevant to the request.

3). UFO report made by PCs Porter, Turnpenny and Baxter on 21 November
1980. This is not the date specified by the applicant but the report is identical
in all other details and | believe this is the one he is seeking. A copy is
attached. The name of the individual at West Yorkshire Police who sent the
report to the MOD has been removed as it is not relevant to the request.

4). We have no UFO report from PC Alan Godfrey on 29 November 1980.
These papers consist of all the known available documents on these
incidents. The files for this period have been prepared for release to The

National Archives and although we can not be sure when TNA will decide to
collect them, it is likely to be only a few months before they will be fully open

i _ | “= no reason not to release copies of these documents to

A draft letter is attached.
Authorisation

| hereby give authorisation for the release of the aforementioned information
to the Applicant.

Dear|

Further to my e-mails of 21° and 22™4 September, I am now in a position to give you a substantive
reply to your remaining four Freedom of Information requests about ‘unidentified flying object’
sightings reported in 1979 and 1980. I have examined our surviving UFO report and
correspondence files for this period and attached to this letter are copies of all the documents I
have been able to locate which are relevant to your requests. Where appropriate information
which is not relevant to your requests has been removed.

26 January 1979 — 2045 Denbigh, North Wales

UFO sighting report made by PC Hughes and witnessed by PC Berwyn Jones on 26 January 1979
at 2033. Also two further reports from a member of the public around the same time. Please note
these two reports are essentially the same report which was originally made to Denbigh Police and
copied to the London Air Traffic Control Centre (Military).

Late 1979 — Dumfries, Scotland

We have no UFO sighting reports relevant to your request in late 1979. I have however, located a
report received from a member of the public from Dumfries and witnessed by PC Smith, plus five
other PCs on 30 August 1980. I have included a copy for your information.

29 November 1980 - 0400 Moors Keighley, Todmorden Area, West Yorkshire

UFO sighting reported by PC Porter, PC Turnpenny and PW Baxter at 0449 on 21 November
1980. Although the date of this report does not match your request, the report matches your
description in all other aspects and I believe this might be the report you are seeking.

29 November 1980 — 0500-0550 Todmorden, West Yorkshire
We have no record of having received such a report from PC Alan Godfrey.

Thope this is useful. If this information does not address your requirements or you wish to
complain about any aspect of the handling of these requests, then you should contact the
undersigned in the first instance. Should you remain dissatisfied, then you may apply for an
internal review by contacting the Director of Information Exploitation, 6th Floor, MOD Main
Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB.
If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the

@ Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD
internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website,

http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely,

REDACTION ON ORIGINAL DOCUMENT

Dear Sir

I enclose for your information a copy of a report,
about an incident which occurred in the Halifax
Division of this Force on.21 November 1980.

aN

Yours faithfully,

Senior Administrative Officer

The Ministry of Defence
Dept. S 4 (Air)

Main Building
Whitehall

London

SWLA 2HB

0M Ch. Supt. A Division (Halifax)

TO Force Control

U.F.0, SIGHTING

At 0449Hrs. 21.11.80. PC 2624 Porter Dog Section

PC 4418 TURNPENNY Ovenden Police Station, Halifax

and PW 5117 Baxter Halifax Police Station. Saw very
bright steel blue flashing light moving from NORTH to
SOUTH and then from a WESTERLY to an EASTERLY direction
high in the sky.

Two different locations. (1) Oxenhope from the Withins
Moor at Halifax. (2) Keighley from the direction of
The Withins Public House, Halifax.

Light last seen by all three officers to go high in the
sky in the general direction of Keighley from Halifax
moving from left to right. Movements over a great
distance and then moved towards Keighley and disappeared.

Air traffic control contacted, they have no record of
aircraft in the area nor have they tracked anything at all.

- All other-emergency services contacted with negative —

result.
All other police areas contacted negative results.

Directorate of Air Staff - Freedom of Information

MINISTRY OF DEFENCE

5" Floor, Zone H, Main Building, Whitehall, London SW1A 2H

(Switchboard) 020 7218 9000

Qur Reference
D/DAS/10/2/8/13
Hessle

East Yorkshire 31” August 2005

Der

I am writing to provide a substantive response to your Freedom of Information request in which
you asked for six separate pieces of information. I will address these in the same order as your
request. I apologise for the length of time it has taken to provide these documents

1. Please find enclosed a copy of file D/Sec (AS)12/5 - UFOs, Close encounter reports, Alien
entities, Abductions etc, as requested. Names and private addresses have been removed as they
are not relevant to your request.

2. Please find enclosed a copy of correspondence between the Under Secretary of State (RAF) and
John Ellis MP in July 1977 concerning a Press report of an unidentified object seen on
Humberside in May 1977. These are all the papers we are aware of still held by the MOD. Our
UFO report files for this period are now open for viewing at The National Archives (TNA). They
can be found under The National Archives reference of DEFE 71/34 (UFO Reports 1 Jan 1977 —
31 May 1977) and DEFE 71/35 (UFO Reports 1 June 1977 — 30 September 1977). Details of how
to access records at TNA can be found on their website at www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

3. Please find enclosed copies of documents held by the MOD concerning a report of a incident
on the 21 April 1991 concerning an Alitalia jet. You will notice that this was an ‘Occurrence
Report’ not a ‘Airmiss’ and it occurred over the Kent coast, not the North Sea. However, the CAA
have confirmed that there were no Airmiss reported on this date and given the details you
provided we believe this is the information you are seeking. Information that is not relevant to
your request has been removed.

4. Please find enclosed a copy of the CAA Airmiss Report No.2/95 concerning an Airmiss on 6
January 1995. This was produced by the CAA and a copy is held by the MOD. The CAA have
confirmed that they are content for us to release this document to you.

5. You requested papers including analysis by Defence Intelligence Staff of a UFO report and
photograph taken over Craigluscar Reservoir near Dunfermline, Fife on 19 February 1994. Please
find enclosed a copy of a report which was sent to the MOD by The Scottish Daily Record who
had been contacted by a member of the public. Our records show that Air defence staff were
consulted at the time and they reported that there had been no unusual radar returns. Defence
Intelligence Staff were also contacted, but we have no record of a formal reply. There is no

indication that this sighting produced anything of defence concern. The photographs are not
@eontainea in our files and we assume they were returned to the Scottish Daily Record.

6. You requested papers including analysis by Defence Intelligence Staff of a UFO report and
photograph taken at Calvine, Tayside on 4 August 1990. Please find attached a copy of the details
received by the MOD. Our records show that a number of photographs were taken and passed to
The Scottish Daily Record, who in turn sent them to the MOD. The photographs showed a large
stationary, diamond-shaped object past which, it appeared a small jet aircraft was flying. These
were considered by the relevant staff within the Department who have responsibility for air
defence matters and they concluded that the jet aircraft was a Harrier. No definite conclusion was
reached regarding the diamond-shaped object. The photographs were returned to the Scottish
Daily Record.

Thope this is helpful. If this information does not address your requirements or you wish to
complain about any aspect of the handling of this request, then you should contact the
undersigned in the first instance. Should you remain dissatisfied, then you may apply for an
internal review by contacting the Director of Information Exploitation, 6th Floor, MOD Main
Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB.

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD
internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website,
http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk

Yours sincerely,

request in which you asked for six separate pieces of information. | will

apologies for the delay in sending this response.

1. Please find enclosed a copy of file D/Sec (AS)12/5 - UFOs, Close
encounter reports, Alien entities, Abductions etc, as requested. Names and
private addresses have been removed as they are not relevant to your
request.

Secretary of State (RAF) and John Ellis MP in July 1977 concerning a Press
report of an unidentified object seen on Humberside in May 1977. These are
all the papers we are aware of still held by the MOD, but you may wish to be
aware that our UFO report files for this period are now open for viewing at
The Nationa! Archives (TNA). They can be found under The National Archives
reference of DEFE 71/34 (UFO Reports 1 Jan 1977 — 31 May 1977) and
DEFE 71/35 (UFO Reports 1 June 1977 — 30 September 1977). Details of
how to access records at TNA can be found on their website at
www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

3. Please find enclosed copies of documents held by the MOD concerning a
report of a incident on the 21 April 1991 concerning an Alitalia jet. You will
notice that this was an ‘Occurrence Report’ not a ‘Airmiss’ and it occurred
over the Kent coast, not the North Sea. However, the CAA have confirmed
that there were no Airmiss reported on this date and given the details you
provided we believe this is the information you are seeking. Information that
is not relevant to your request has been removed.

4. Please find enclosed a copy of the CAA Airmiss Report No.2/95
concerning an Airmiss on 6 January 1995. This was produced by the CAA and
a copy is held by the MOD. The CAA have confirmed that they are content for
us to release this document to you.

5. You requested papers including analysis by Defence Intelligence Staff of a
UFO report and photograph taken over Craigluscar Reservoir near
Dunfermline, Fife on 19 February 1994. Please find enclosed a copy of a
report which was sent to the MOD by The Scottish Daily Record who had
been contacted by a member of the public. Our records show that Air defence
staff were consulted at the time and they reported that there had been no
unusual radar returns. Defence Intelligence Staff were also contacted, but we
have no record of a formal reply. There is no indication that this sighting
produced anything of defence concern. The photographs are not contained in
our files and we assume they were returned to the Scottish Daily Record.
6. You requested papers including analysis by Defence Intelligence Staff of a
UFO report and photograph taken at Calvine, Tayside on 4 August 1990.
Please find attached a copy of the details received by the MOD. Our records
show that a number of photographs were taken and passed to The Scottish
Daily Record, who in turn sent them to the MOD. The photographs showed a
large stationary, diamond-shaped object past which, it appeared a small jet
aircraft was flying. These were considered by the relevant staff within the
Department who have responsibility for air defence matters and they
concluded that the jet aircraft was a Harrier. No definite conclusion was
reached regarding the diamond-shaped object. The photographs were
returned to the Scottish Daily Record.

What information has been requested? (please state the exact wording of the request):

| wonder if it is possible to request the following items in a Freedom of Information
request and whether they would fit into the current allotment?

1. File put together by Nick Pope in 1992 on Close Encounters, Alien Abductions.
2. Ministerial correspondence about UFO sightings on Humberside in May 1977.

3. Copies of all MOD, RAF and CAA papers on the 21 April 1991 UFO 'nearmiss' with
Alitalia jet over the North Sea.

4. Copies of all MOD, RAF and CAA papers on the 6 January 1995 'nearmiss' with
BA jet descending into Manchester Airport.

5. All papers including analysis by Defence Intelligence Staff of UFO report and
photograph taken by a Craigluscar Reservoir near Dunfermline, Fife,

19 February 1994.

6. All papers including Defence intelligence Staff analysis of UFO photograph taken by
two members of the public at Calvine, Tayside on 4 August 1990 and passed to MOD
for analysis.
If the Clearing House advises that information should be released, do you think it would be suitable for
simultaneous release? :

Please provide a summary of the case:

This applicant has corresponded with the MOD before about UFOs and on this occasion has
requested 6 separate pieces of information. Requests 1, 2, 5 and 6 only concern the MOD and
we are currently searching for the relevant information.

Requests 3 and 4 however, also request information from the Civil Aviation Authority who
should be consulted about release of any information they hold on these incidents. Both of
these events invoived civil aircraft which reported objects flying past their aircraft. One was
reported as an “air miss” (Airmiss Report 2/95) and the other was a “Mandatory Occurrence
Report” (MOR 199101151). On both occasions the CAA investigated the incidents and failed to
identify what was seen. Because of this, a link has been made with UFOs and the MOD have had
enquiries in the past about the incident in 1995. A copy of the CAA report was obtained at the
time in order to answer these enquiries and remains on our files. We are not familiar with the
details of events in 1991, but the UK Airprox Board have given me the brief outline. It is not clear
at this stage how much information the MOD is likely to hold on these events. The CAA
regularly publish Air miss reports, but | do not know whether MOR’s are published.

Case History:

The file requested at 1) has been requested from the TNT archives. When this arrives we should
be able to release this information providing personal data is redacted in accordance with
DPA9S8.

The whereabouts of the file containing the information requested at 2) is uncertain at present. It
is either still with MOD or just transferred to The National Archives. Info-Records to check next
week.

Regarding 3) and 4) as detailed above. We will see what information the MOD holds on these.
Regarding 5) | have located the report and the LM to DIS about the report and photographs.
There is no reply from DIS on our files. The DIS policy files of the time contain no details and
their other files are part of the documents stored in the asbestos contaminated archive which are
currently unaccessable.

Finally, as for 6) we have sent for our files for the period from TNT and will pursue our enquiries
on these when they arrive.

Please state which exemption(s)/exception(s) may apply to this request and why?:

1am currently not aware of any exemptions which may apply to this request.

Is there a possibility that a certificate under either s.23 or $.24 of the FOI Act will be issued at this
stage?:

NO.

Why are you making a referral?:

The applicant has made a request for information held by the Civil Aviation Authority as well as
the MOD concerning two of these requests.

Does this case need to the referred to the National Security Liaison Group (NSLG)? i.e. might s23/24
be used?;

i

We have had a number of recent enquiries about the contents of the UFO files

held by the Ministry of nee, and I thought it would be useful if I put you
in the picture on what we old.

Our files are made up of UFO sighting reports, together with enquiries about
other aspects of the UFO phenomenon, and the position of the Ministry of
Defence. Virtually all of the UFO sighting reports we receive relate to lights
or discs in thé sky, or various ill-defined shapes or objects. In my time here
I have not received a single report of any close encounter, but we have, in the
Past, received a very small number of letters that might fall into the category
of a close encounter of the third or fourth kind. I recently had a request from
a researcher to release these reports. As we receive so few, and recognising
their value to researchers, I agreed that these reports could be released. [I
also said that I would make this material available to those UFO groups that we
refer witnesses and enthusiasts to, and I therefore attach this for your
information. As is usual when releasing UFO Treports, I have deleted information
relating to the identity of the witnesses, together with other personal
information. I draw no conclusions from this material, and remain open-minded
as _to what may have prompted these five reports. I have not included material
relating to reports that are already in the public domain, such as photocopied
extracts from books such as "Above Top Secret".

I will keep you informed about any such cases in the future, and - as usual ~
will encourage any witness to get in touch with you. I remain of the opinion,
however, that reports of close encounters are more likely to be reported to
organisations such as yours than to us.

More generally, we are able to answer specific queries you may have about
particular sightings which have occurred recently, and can let you know whether
we have a report on the incident, and if So, what it contains. We will also do
our best to answer any specific questions we are asked, and can also give
general information about our files, and what they contain. However, the files
that we hold only go back to 1985, with previous files having been "archived",
and sent to the Public Record Office. Because these files are covered by the
"30 year rule", and as, prior to 1967, UFO files were destroyed after five

years, files held in this office are likely to be the only accessible UFO files
at present,

I hope this is helpful.

PARLIAMENTARY UNDER-SECRETARY OF STATE
FOR DEFENCE FOR THE ROYAL AIR FORCE

AP/JW 234/79 1 July 19977

Dea boka,
ae aSked me to reply to your letter of 31st May

concerning the Press report of an unidentified object seen recently
on the Humberside. I am sorry I have not written before but as so
often happens with reports like this the invesitgation has been
inconclusive. .

I should explain that the Department has not the resources to
conduct an independent scientific study into the nature of
unidentified flying objects. We invariably pass reports of sighting
to the specialist staffs responsible for the air defence of the
country but it is not possible to pursue enquiries from the public ¢
the point of positive identification. You will appreciate that some
reports are necessarily vague and we feel it would not be gjustifiabl
to ask the staffs to make elaborate enquiries if in their opinion
these would not be necessary in the interests of defence.

The Department have reached that point with the sightings you
referred to in your letter. The five reports received by RAF Binbre
show a marked lack of consistency when one seeks to correlate then
into a group of events. Three are incomplete and lack significant
information such as the size of the object and its distance relative
to known landmarks. They refer variously to quite a small silver
triangular shape, to a circular red object 25 feet in diameter, and
three objects, round in shape, with two as big and as bright as a
star and the third larger and flashing. The other two reports were
from Mr Roy Thomson and Dale Roe who are named in the press cutting.
There are certain similarities here. Both refer to an object
measuring 20 feet by 3 feet which was either oval or cigar~shaped;
but the cutting mentions a noise like a whirlwind while the report
held by Binbrook records Dale Roe as saying there was no sound at al

I am afraid the Department can offer ‘no positive explanation for the
objects, nor why they gave rise to five separate reports. The
descriptions given do not appear to refer to meteorological phenomen
The report of the three objects, one of which was flashing, could be
a description of an aircraft travelling at night, possibly with
landing lights on. Flashing lights are standard aircraft equipment
and are carried above and below the fuselage. Another possibility i

/that balloons
dohn Ellis Esa MP

House of Commdns
London SWIA OAA
that balloons were seen. The Meteorological Office were not

flying balloons at the times and places reported but other
organisations, such as universities, sometimes use similar equipmen
in experimental work and it may even be that balloons released

by foreign users on the Continent, or from ships in the North Sea,
reached the Humberside although we have no knowledge of this.

It is all rather speculative and I am sorry I cannot be more

le Saal,

concerning the Press report of an unidentified object seen recently
on the Humberside. I am sorry I have not written before but as so
often happens with reports like this the invesitgation has been

inconclusive.

I should explain that the Department has not the resources to
conduct an independent scientific study into the nature of
unidentified flying objects. We invariably pass reports of sighting:

country but it is not possible to pursue enquiries from the public t
the point of positive identification. You will appreciate that some

The Department have reached that point with the sightings you
referred to in your letter. The five reports received by RAF Binbrox
show a marked lack of consistency when one seeks to correlate then
into a group of events. Three are incomplete and lack significant
information such as the sige of the object and its distance relative
to known landmarks. They refer variously to quite a small silver
triangular shape, to a circular red object 25 feet in diameter, and +

star and the third larger and flashing. The other two reports were
from Mr Roy Thomson and Dale Koe who are named in the press cutting.
There are certain similarities here. Both refer to an object
measuring 20 feet by 3 feet which was either oval or cigar-shaped;
but the cutting mentions a noise like a whirlwind while the report
held by Binbrook records Dale Roe as saying there was no sound at all

-I am afraid the Department can offer no positive explanation for the
objects, nor why they gave rise to five separate reports. The
descriptions given do not appear to refer to meteorological phenomens
The report of the three objects, one of which was flashing, could be
a description of an aircraft travelling at night, possibly with

@.... from US of SCBAP) to John Ellis, Esq, MP ~

Fred Malley asked me to reply to your letter of 31st May concerning the Press

©

report of an unidentified object seen recently on the Humberside. I om sorry
I have not written before but as se often happens with reports like this the

investigation has been inconclusive.

I should explain that the Department has not the resources to conduct an

independent scientific study into the nature of unidentified flying objects.

'
Pa

We invariably pass reports of sightings to the specialist staffs responsible fo

ull

the air defences of the country but it is not possible to pursue enquiries from

the public to the point of positive identification. You will appreciate that
some reports are necessarily vague and we feel it would not be justifiable to
ask the staffs to make elaborate enquiries if in their opinion these would not

be necessary in the interests of defence.

The Department have reached that point with the sightings you referred to in
your letter. The five reports received by RAF Binbrook show a marked lack of
consistency when one seeks to correlate them into a group of events. Three are
incomplete and lack significant information such as the size of the object and
its distance relative to known landmarks. They refer variously to quite a small
silver triangular shape, to a circular red object 25 feet in diameter, and to
three objects, round in shape, with two as big and as bright as a'star and the
third larger and flashing. The other two reports were from Mr Roy Thomson and
Dale Roe who are named in the press cutting. There are certain similiarities
here. Both refer to an object measuring 20 feet by 3 feet which was either oval
or cigar~shaped; but the cutting mentione a noise like a whirlwind while the

report held by Binbrook records Dale Roe as saying there was no sound at all.

pity
=

Tem afraid the Department can offer no enation for the objects,

4
is

y they gave'rise to five separa

Pa

e reports. The descriptions given do not
appear to refer to meteorologicel phenomena. The report of the three objects,
one of which was flashing, could be a description of an aircraft travelling at
night, possibly with landing lights on. Flashing lights are standard aircraft
equipment and are carried above and below the fuselage.« Another possibility is
that balleons were seen. The Meteorological Office were not flying balloons at
the times and places reported but other organisations, such as universities,
sometimes use similar equipment in experimental work and it may even be that
balloons released by foreign users on the Continent, or from ships in the Nerth

Sea, reached the Humberside although we have no knowledge of this.

It is all rather speculative and I am sorry I cannot be more helpful.

CAA REPORT OF AIR INCIDENT NEAR LYDD ON 21 APRIL 1991

References: A) Letter from GREED cated 6 May 1991 j

B) Article from ‘The Independent’ newspaper dated 6 May 1991
C) CAA Incident Report dated 25 April 1991 oe

1. Please find attached a copy of the CAA report of an air incident reported by
an AlItalia MD80 aircraft on 21 April near Lydd in Kent (Reference C). I also
attach copies of References A and B. oo
2. Given the MoD spokesman’s published denial of any UK military involvement, I
believe that the radar evidence attached to the report which shows an object in
the vicinity of the AlItalia aircraft should be examined (in the first instance)
in the context of an air defence/air safety matter rather than as a UFO report.

3. I would be appreciative if GE3(RAF) (consulting with DI55 as appropriate)
could provide the CAA with the assistance they have requested in their covering
note to the report. I also believe that it would be more appropriate, in this
case, for Sec(AS)la to undertake to ansver ST: letter as the questions
he has posed are not strictly UFO-related.

4. I would be grateful for sight of any conclusions which GE3(RAF) may draw
from his enquiries, as if no firm conclusions can be drawn, it will be necessary
to reappraise our approach to this sighting.

Dear Sir/Madam,

I am writing to you with the hope that you may be«able to help me. I
have seen various press reports ( Daily Telegraph, Guardian, Daily
Star ) whieh refer to the sighting of an object described as being

3 metres in length and cylindrical. This object was apparently ob-
~served at approximately 22000 feet on April 21 around 9pm by Captain
Achille Zaghetti who was flying en route from Milan to Heathrow. The
sighting apparently occurred over Kent. My reason for writing is to
enguire if you can help on several points.

1. Has there been an official MOD investigation of this case, or has
the MOD assisted in an official Civilian Aviation Authority invest-
-igation?

2. Can you cenfirm that the MOD does not have an air-to-surface missile
which could go to 22000 feet?

3. Is the Rapier missile only launched from Aberporth, Wales and Tain in
Scotland, and is its limit around 10000 feet ?

4. Could the sighting be connected with the firing range at lydd ?

5. Is it possible to obtain copies of any records relating to this case
which may have been forwarded to the MOD? 4s I understand it a tracking
was made at West Drayton, Is it possible to obtain copies of any final
reports pertaining to this case?

Should you be able to help in any way it would be very mush appreciated
as this is just for my own interest. Thankyou for taking the time to read
my letter. I have enclosed a SAE.

Yours Faithfully,
Inquiry into pilot’s |
sighting of missile |

THE CIVIL Aviation Authority
has launched an investigation into
a reported near-collision between
a passenger airliner and a missile
in civilian airspace over Kent.

But the Ministry of Defence
said it could not have been a Brit-
ish missile. The CAA says that
there were no other aircraft under
its control there and that it has
still failed to explain the sighting.
_ Captain Achille Zaghetti, pilot
of the McDonnell Douglas MD80,
operated by the Italian airline
Alitalia, was flying from Milan to
Heathrow ..at 22,200ft, with 57
people on board, on 21 April when
he reported to British Air Traffic
' Control that a missile had flown
less than 1,000 feet above him in
the opposite direction. :

It was 9pm, still light in the air,
and the local air traffic controllers
at Lydd airport said the visibility
was exceptionally good.

Capt Zaghetti said the missile
was light brown and about 3 me-
tres.tong. His co-pilot confirmed
the sighting. Their descriptions
are consistent with a target missile
used for artillery or air defence
practice, but the Ministry of De-
fence insisted yesterday that none
were in use on 21 April.

Although it has taken the re-
port of an experienced pilot at
face value, in a public statement
yesterday the CAA attempted to
imply that he had seen a drifting
Object, perhaps a stray balloon.

. The Independent’

By Stephen Ward

The statement said: “The pilot
said the object was light brown,
round, 3 metres long,.and did not
describe any means of propulsion.
The aircraft was under the control
of the London air traffic control
centre who had no other aircraft
in the vicinity but consistent with
the pilot report, a faint radar trace
was observed 10 nautical miles be-
hind the Alitalia aircraft.

“The air traffic controller sub-

mitted an occurrence report and +

investigatory action began imme-
diately. Extensive inquiries have
failed to provide any indication of
what the sighting may have been.”
- A Ministry of Defence spokes-
man said yesterday: “Whatever he

“might have seen might have been

something that was flying, but was
certainly not anything that was

-fired. It was a Sunday. The only

ranges we have in the Kent area
are Lydd and Hythe, and they are
concerned with small arms only.”

Asked if target missiles’ were ©

used, he said: “It’s absolutely in
the middle of the busiest air traffic
area. People just don’t fire mis-
siles there, but of course, we do
have quite a few UFO reports and
often people who see these things
describe them as missile or cigar-
shaped, or else round, and some-
times they do appear to be travel-
ling with no'’means of propulsion.”

6 Nay PA

CAA REPORT OF AIR INCIDENT NEAR LYDD ON 21 APRIL 1991
Reference: D/Sec(AS)8/1 dated 21 May 1991

1. You asked for assistance with your reply to a letter from a
member of the public on the above subject.

2. Rapier is a short range air defence missile with an
operational ceiling of around 10,000 feet

It could go
higher, but not as high as 22,000 feet. I don't Know if Lance
could get up that high but it is considerable larger than 3
metres, and I think it is safe to assume that we have not been
firing tactical nuclear missiles over the Kent coast! ’

3. Surface to air missiles are fired at only two Army ranges in

UK: Manorbier in South Wales (for low level air defence weapons

only) and Benbecula in the Western Isles.

4. Lydd is one of a number of small training areas on the Kent

coast that make up part of the Cinque Ports Training Area (CPTA).

It presen rererenrom cere ed
has anti-tank, light mortar, grenade and

rifle ranges as well’ ‘as limited field firing facilities.

Our reference D/Sec(AS)12/3
Walsall .

Date 2 suly 1991

Door

Thank you for your letter of 6 May 1991 regarding the sighting of
an object over Kent on 21 April.

I can confirm that MoD has been asked to assist with the Civil
Aviation Authority examination of the Air Incident report made by
the Alitalia MD80 aircraft’s pilot.

The only surface-to-air missiles that could go as high as 22,000
feet are the Bloodhound (operated by the RAF) and the Sea Dart
(operated by the Royal Navy). The Rapier missile is launched
from the range at Benbecula in the Western Isles and the
Aberporth range in Wales. _
Rapier missiles could not go
as high as 22,000 feet. The sighting in question would not be
connected with the range at Lydd, as this has only anti-tank,

light mortar, grenade and rifle ranges as well as limited field
firing facilities.

It will not be possible for MoD to send you any of the
correspondence relating to the Alitalia pilot’s Air Incident

report. However, you may wish to contact the Civil Aviation
Authority separately.

Yoo Racy

From: QD Secretariat (Air Staff)2a, Room 8245.

I hope that this is helpful.

Middlesex UB10 ORZ Date 2 July 1991

Do SE

Thank you for your letter of 21 May 1991 regarding the report of
an air incident on Sunday 21 April 1991. 1 am sorry it has taken
me so long to respond.

I can confirm that the Alitalia MD80 captain’s report and the
LATCC controllers’ report have been examined by those
departments within MoD responsible for the air defence of the UR.
These departments have not been able to confirm the identity of
the object sighted by the Alitalia MD80 crew.

However, we have been able to rule out any possibility that what
was seen was a missile from Army firing ranges in the Lydd area.
Additionally, we have no reports of any space related activity
which could be used to provide an explanation for what was seen,
and the description of the object given by the Aliitalia captain
does not correspond with what would be expected if the object had
been a meteorological balloon.

In the absence of any clear evidence which could be used to
identify the object it is our intention to treat this sighting

like that of any other Unidentified Flying Object and therefore
we will not be undertaking any further investigation into the

sighting.

From: QM Secretariat (Air Staff)2a, Room 8245.

I hope that this is helpful.

ATC OCCURRENCE REPORT - REPORT OF MISSILE TYPE OBJECT -
21 91

Reference:
A. CAA 91/01151 dated 10 May 91.

1. At Reference you requested military comment on the title
Occurrence Report.

2. All the information was passed to the MOD and was examined
by the departments responsible for the air defence of the UK.
We are advised that those departments have not been able to
confirm the identity of the object sighted by the Alitalia MD80
crew.

3. The MOD were able to rule out the possibility that the
object was a missile from the Army firing ranges in the Lydd
area. Additionally, MOD has no report of any space related
activity which could provide an explanation, and the description
does not correspond with that expected if the object had been a
meteorological balloon.

4. In the absence of any clear evidence which could be used to
identify. the object, we are informed that MOD will treat this
sighting like that of any other Unidentified Flying Object and
therefore will not be able to undertake any further investigation
into the sighting. We now propose that this Occurrence Report -
is closed.

10th June 1991.

Mr Tom King M.P.,

The Secretary of State for Defence,
Ministry of Defence,

Whitehall,

London.

Dear Mr King,

I refer to an incident reported in ’The Sunday Times’ dated 5th
May 1991, in which it was alledged that an unidentified ‘missile’
almost collided with an Alitalia passenger airliner en route from
Milan to London. It was also reported that the near miss occurred
almost directly above a Ministry of Defence firing range. I
understand, however, that the Ministry of Defence has stated that
the object had not originated from any British armed forces. The
corollary of this statement is that some (assumedly unauthorised)
agency is launching projectiles in British airspace, thereby
creating a very serious threat to civilian air traffic.

I have conducted some personal research into reports of such
unidentified incursions into our airspace and it appears that
this is by no means an isolated incident.

I should like to know the findings of investigations into this
matter carried out by the Ministry of Defence, and what actions
Her Majesty’s Government intend to take to ensure the safety of
aircraft. JI urge you to authorise a public enquiry into this and
the many similar cases of such intrusions into British airspace.

A copy of this letter has been sent to my member of Parliament,
Mr Paul Murphy M.P.

Yovirs sincerel

CAA REPORT OF AIR INCIDENT NEAR LYDD ON 21 APRIL 1991

Reference: D/Sec(AS)12/2 dated 9 May 1991

1. In response to $y; letter dated 6 May 1991 I

would suggest the following responses to his questions 2, 3 and
4, which relate to ranges and missile capabilities:

Question 2 (I assune i means surface to air missiles)

The only surface to air missiles that could go as high as 22,000
feet are the Bloodhound (operated by the RAF) and the Sea Dart
(operated by the Royal Navy).

Question 3

The Rapier missile is launched from the range at Benbecula in the
Western Isles and the Aberporth range in Wales. Precise details
of the operational ceiling of Rapier missiles is classified, but
they could not go as high as 22,000 feet.

Question 4
The sighting in question would not be connected with the range at

Lydd, which only has anti-tank, light mortar, grenade and rifle
ranges, as well as limited field firing facilities.

2. I have attached the information that I received, in case

you need additional details. -

Dear

I am writing concerning your message dated 6 January in which you made a request under the
Freedom of Information Act for information about Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) sightings.
Your request has been passed to this departm we are the focal point within the Ministry of
Defence for correspondence about UFOs.

First you requested the findings of an MOD investigation into an alleged UFO sighting above
Craigluscar Reservoir near Dunfermline, Fife. Our records show that The Scottish Daily Record
contacted the MOD in February 1994 concerning a UFO sighting report and some photographs
that they had been sent by a member of the public. The individual reported travelled to
Craigluscar Reservoir on the afternoon of 19" February 1994 to take some photographs of the
Reservoir to assist with a painting of the area. He heard a humming noise, turned towards the
Reservoir and saw a disc-like object, high in the sky, moving slowly in his direction. He described
the object as metallic, with several points of diffused light on its underside, inside a darker
coloured rim. He took two photographs before the object moved away to the west. Air defence
staff were consulted at the time and records show that there had been no unusual radar returns.
There is no indication that this sighting produced anything of defence concern. The photographs
are not contained in our files and we assume they were returned to the Scottish Daily Record.

You also requested a photograph of a UFO near Pitlochry, Perthshire in 1990. The MOD does not
produce photographs of UFO sightings. The only photographs the MOD holds are those which
members of the public very occasionally send to us with their sighting reports. If requested these
are returned to the individual, or if they do not want them returned, they are filed with the report
in our files. We have examined our records for 1990 and they contain no photographs relevant to
your request. However, we have a record that two members of the public contacted The Scottish
Daily Record to report a large diamond shape UFO hovering for about 10 minutes before
ascending vertically upwards at high speed. The witnesses said that during the sighting an RAF
aircraft believed to be a Harrier was in the area. The sighting was over Calvine, 20 miles north of
Pitlochry at 2100 hours on 4 August 1990. A number of colour photographs were taken and
passed to The Scottish Daily Record, who in turn sent the negatives to the MOD. These were
considered at the time and it was concluded that the jet was a Harrier. No definite conclusions
were reached regarding large diamond-shaped object. Our records show that the original negatives
were returned to The Scottish Daily Record.

28 Feb 1994

D155 - ST

UFO SIGHTING OVER CRAIGLUSCAR RESERVOIR

1. I was approached last week by a reporter from the Scottish Daily Record,
who asked my advice with regard to some UFO photographs that had been sent in by
a member of the public. I subsequently managed to acquire these photographs,
together with a statement from the witness, and some sketches that he had made.
I have attached this material for your consideration.

2. I have spoken to the witness, a
The following details, which were not in his original statement, may be of use:

- He appeared genuine, and rather disappointed at the way in which the paper
eventually ran the story, which he saw as frivolous (I do not have a copy of
this, but believe it was printed today). He did not appear to know anything
about UFOs, and aside from his own experience, expressed no interest in the
subject. I do not believe that he sought or received any payment from the

newspaper. a

- The camera used was an old SLR model, a Chinon CS. The film used was a 200
ASA.

- The alleged incident took place at approximately 3.15pm.

- He estimated that the object was approximately the size of a jumbo jet, and
came down to a height of around 300 feet AGL.

3. It seems to me that this case is either a hoax, or something very
interesting indeed.

4. The Daily Record have already asked me for my views, and I have deployed |
the standard line that our initial consideration of the photos has produced no
obvious explanation, but that we are now having a more detailed look. As word
of this case spreads (Some of the UFO lobby are already aware of the story) we
may come under increasing pressure.

5. I would be grateful for your views on this case, with particular
reference to the photograph of. the object at close range.

On the afternoon of Saturday, 719th. February, 1994, I drove to Craigluscar
Reservoir, just outside Dunfermline, in order to take some photographs of that
avea. I am an amateur artist, and wanted the photographs to assist me in producing
a painting of the reservoir. I am a member of the local engling club who stock and
have the fishing rights of this water.:Already having taken photographs there in
the summer, I wanted to have several of the landscape in winter.

I took a number of. photographs. ag,J.welked along the bank of the reservoir,
and was locking eastwards at the furthest point of the reservoir, and pondering
whether I should take some photographs looking east, away from the water. I became
aware of what I can only describe as e,kind of humming noise, such as that from
high voltage power lines: I also felt very, uneasy, and turned slowly towards the

reservoir.

feirly high in the air, and coming slowly in my direction, was a disc-
like abject, which was definitely an aircraft. I was holding my camera in my hand,
but felt unable to use it. I have never experienced . such a feeling before;.I did
not THINK of taking a shot, In retrospect, it seems there must have been some kind
of compulsion. I was concentrating on the craft, yet not really absorbing what was
happening. At that point, I was very afraid. But I now realise that the fear
passed, and was repleced by a sense of "geldxed resignation”. (I don't have. better
words to describe it.)

I later realised that I must have, been looking at whatever it was, for more
than fifteen minutes. The craft came .close enough for me to see thet it was
definitely metallic, and had several points of diffused light on its underside,

inside a darker coloured “rim",

As the craft began to move away, all sense of the feelings which had stayec
my hand, disappeared. I raised my camera, and took two photographs. The craft's
acealeration was phenomenal - By the time.I'd wound the film on between the twc
shots, it was a mere dot in the sky to the west. There was no noise at all, apart

from the "buzzing" when it was near to,me.

1 knaw nothing at all about "U.F.0.'s", snd the subject does not interest me. Bui
nothing of what I saw or experienced, was "in my imagination". of that, IT ar
certain. Leter that day, I telephoned R.A.F. Piteavie, to enquire as to whethe!
there had been any unusual eir activity that day, I was told there had not been. -
am an aircraft enthusiast myself, and pride myself in their recognition. I kno

thet whet I saw was no conventional aircreft.

DDPR(RAF )

PHOTOGRAPHS OF AN UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECT - SCOTTISH DAILY RECORD

1. US of S(AF) may wish to be aware that the Scottish Daily Record may run
a story regarding an alleged sighting of a UFO near Pitlochrie in early August.
Such stories are not normally drawn to the attention of Ministers and the MoD
press office invariably responds to questions along well-established lines
emphasising our limited interest in the UFO phenomenon and explaining that we
therefore do not have the resources to undertake any in-depth investigations
into particular sightings. On this occasion however, the MoD has been provided
with six photographic negatives of an alleged UFO by the Scottish Daily Record
and has been asked for comments almost certainly for inclusion in a forthcoming
story. For this reason it is felt that US of S(AF) should be made aware of the
background and the line adopted by the DDPR(RAF) in responding to the newspaper.

2. The photographs, which were received on 10 Sept, are alleged to have
been taken near the A9 road at Clavine, north of Pitlochrie on the evening of 4
August. They show a large stationary, diamond-shaped object past which, it
appears, a small jet aircraft is flying. The negatives have been considered by
the relevant staffs who have established that the jet aircraft is a Harrier (and
also identified a barely visible second aircraft, again probably a Harrier) but
have reached no definite conclusion regarding the large object. It has also
been confirmed that there is no record of Harriers operating in the area at the
time at which the photographs are alleged to have been taken. The negatives
have now been returned to the Scottish Daily Record.

3. In consultation with DDPR(RAF) it has been agreed that the attached
lines to take should be used in responding to the Scottish Daily Record. These
are consistent with the position adopted in the replies to the many public and
occasional parliamentary enquiries on the subject of UFOs.

Sec(AS)2a

MB8245 | |

SCOTTISH DAILY RECORD — PHOTOGRAPHS OF UFO

DEFENSIVE LINES TO TAKE:

- Have looked at photographs, no definite conclusions
reached regarding large diamond-shaped object.

- Confident that jet aircraft is a Harrier.

- Have no record of Harriers operating in location at
stated time/date.

- No other reports received by MoD of unusual air
activity or sightings at location/date/time.

IF PRESSED

Who in MoD studied pictures?

~ All sighting reports (including on occasion
photographs/drawings etc) received by MoD are referred to
the staff in the departments which are responsible for
air defence of UK who examine them as part of their
normal duties.

Other reports of UFOs from Scotland?

- UFO reports from Scotland are rare.

background and the line adopted by the DDPR(RAF) in responding to the newspaper.

2. The photographs, which were received on 10 Sept, are alleged to have
been taken near the A9 road at Clavine, north of Pitlochrie on the evening of 4
August. They show a large stationary, diamond-shaped object past which, it
appears, a small jet aircraft is flying. The negatives have been considered by
the relevant staffs who have established that the jet aircraft is a Harrier (and
also identified a barely visible second aircraft, again probably a Harrier) but
have reached no definite conclusion regarding the large object. It has also
been confirmed that there is no record of Harriers operating in the area at the
time at which the photographs are alleged to have been taken. The negatives
have now been returned to the Scottish Daily Record.

3. In consultation with DDPR(RAF) it has been agreed that the attached
lines to take should be used in responding to the Scottish Daily Record. These
are consistent with the position adopted in the replies to the many public and
occasional parliamentary enquiries on the subject of UFOs.

= AS _ "
SCOTTISH DAILY RECORD - PHOTOGRAPHS OF UFO

DEFENSIVE LINES TO TAKE:

- Have looked at photographs, no definite conclusions
reached regarding large diamond-shaped object.

- Confident that jet aircraft is a Harrier.

~ Have no record of Harriers operating in location at
stated time/date.

~ No other reports received by MoD of unusual air
activity or sightings at location/date/time.

IF PRESSED

Who in MoD studied pictures?

- All sighting reports (including on occasion
photographs/drawings etc) received by MoD are referred to
the staff in the departments which are responsible for
air defence of UK who examine them as part of their
normal duties.

Other reports of UFOs from Scotland?

~ UFO reports from Scotland are rare.

CAA REPORT OF AIR INCIDENT NEAR LYDD ON 21 APRIL 1991
Reference: D/Sec(AS)8/1 dated 21 May 1991

1. You asked for assistance with your reply to a letter from a
member of the public on the above subject.

2. Rapier is a short range air defence missile with an
Operational ceiling of around 10,000 feet (I am advised that this
fact is, strictly speaking, classified RESTRICTED). It could go
higher, but not as high as 22,000 feet. I don't know if Lance
could get up that high but it is considerable larger than 3
metres, and I think it is safe to assume that we have not been
firing tactical nuclear missiles over the Kent coast! ,

3. Surface to air missiles are fired at only two Army ranges in
UK: Manorbier in South Wales (for low level air defence weapons
only) and Benbecula in the Western Isles.

4. Lydd is one of a number of small training areas on the Kent
(coast that make up part of the Cinque Ports Training Area (CPTA).

aoe It [is permanently a}tocated to Northern Tretand tactical-training
\

(again RESTRICTED) andi has anti-tank, light mortar, grenade and

rifle ranges as well’ ‘as limited field firing facilities.

CAA REPORT OF AIR INCIDENT NEAR LYDD ON 21 APRIL 1991

1. Please find attached a letter we have received fromja
member of the public about the above incident. I would be
grateful for your advice on questions 2, 3 and 4. os"

2. Could GS Sec 1d and Sec(NS)b comment on question 2,
assuming that the question actually relates to surface-to-air
missiles.

3. Could GS Sec id and Company Secretary DRA comment on
question 3. I understand that the main area for the launching of
Rapier missiles is actually the RA Range Hebrides, although the
Aberporth range is also used.

4. Could GS Sec 1d comment on question 4, and give a bit of
background information on what activity takes place at Lydd.

5. Whilst the Air Force department is already looking into
this incident, any information or suggestions that addresses are
able to provide would be most. welcome!

6. I would be grateful for responses by cop Wednesday 12
June.

6 May 1991.

Dear Sir/Madam,

I am writing to you with the hope that you my be able to help me. I
have geen various press reports ( Daily Telegraph, Guardian, Daily
Star ) whieh refer to the sighting of an object described as being
3 metres in length and cylindrical. This object was apparently ob-
cgerved at approximately 22000 feet on April 21 around 9pm by Captain

Achiit

ile Zaghetti who was flying en route from Milan to Heathrow. The
sighting aprarently occurzed over Kent. My reason for writing is to
enauire if you can help on several points.

l. Has there been an official MOD investigation of this case, or has
the MCD assisted in an official Civilian Aviation Authority invest-
-igation? ;

?
2. Can you confirm that the MCD does not have an air-to-surface nissile
which could go to 22000 feet?

3. Is the Rapier missile only launched from Aberporth, Wales and Tain in
Scotland, and is its limit around 10000 feet ?

4. Could the sighting be connected with the firing range at Lydd ?

5. Is it possible to obtain copies of any records relating to this case
which may have been forwarded to the MOD? As I understand it a tracking
was made at West Drayton. Is it possible to obtain copies of any final
reports pertaining to this case?

Should you be able to help in any way it would be very much appreciated
as this is just for my own interest. Thankyou for taking the time to read
my letter. L have enclosed a SAE.

Yours Faithfully,

Nicholas Redfern.

CAA REPORT OF AIR INCIDENT NEAR LYDD ON 21 APRIL 1991

References: A) Letter from Mr N Redfern dated 6 May 1991 ;
B) Article from ‘The Independent’ newspaper dated 6 May 1991
C) CAA Incident Report dated 25 April 1991 non"

1. Please find attached a copy of the CAA report of an air incident reported by
an AlItalia MD80 aircraft on 21 April near Lydd in Kent (Reference C). I also
attach copies of References A and B. aaa

2. Given the MoD spokesman’s published denial of any UK military involvement, I
believe that the radar evidence attached to the report which shows an object in
the vicinity of the AlItalia aircraft should be examined (in the first instance)
in the context of an air defence/air safety matter rather than as a UFO report.

3. I would be appreciative if GE3(RAF) (consulting with DI55 as appropriate)
could provide the CAA with the assistance they have requested in their covering
note to the report. I also believe that it would be more appropriate, in this
case, for Sec(AS)la to undertake to answer Mr Redfern’s letter as the questions
he has posed are not strictly UFO-related.

4. I would be grateful for sight of any conclusions which GE3(RAF) may draw
from his enquiries, as if no firm conclusions can be drawn, it will be necessary
to reappraise our approach to this sighting.

Sec(AS)2a
MBB245

Inquiry into pilot’s
sighting of missile

' THE CIVIL Aviation Authority

has launched an investigation into
a reported near-collision between
a passenger airliner and a missile
in civilian airspace over Kent.

But the Ministry of Defence
said it could not have been a Brit-
ish missile. The CAA says that
there were no other aircraft under
its control there and that it has
still failed to explain the sighting.
_ Captain Achille Zaghetti, pilot
of the McDonnell Douglas MD80,
operated by the Italian airline

‘Alitalia, was flying from Milan to

Heathrow. at 22,200ft, with 57

- people on board, on 21 April when

he reported to British Air Traffic

| Control that a missile had flown

less than 1,000 feet above him in
the opposite direction.

It was 9pm, still light in the air,
and the local air traffic controllers
at Lydd airport said the visibility
was exceptionally good.

Capt Zaghetti said the missile
was light brown and about 3 me-
tres. long. His co-pilot confirmed
the sighting. Their descriptions
are consistent with a target missile
used for artillery or air defence
practice, but the Ministry of De-
fence insisted yesterday that none
were in use on 21 April.

Although it has taken the re-
port of an experienced pilot at
face value, in a public statement
yesterday the CAA attempted to
imply that he-had seen a drifting
poet perhaps a stray balloon.

‘The Indecendent

By Stephen Ward

The statement said: “The pilot
said the object was light brown,
round, 3 metres long, and did not
describe any means of propulsion.
The aircraft was under the control
of the London air traffic control
centre who had no other aircraft
in the vicinity but consistent with
the pilot report, a faint radar trace
was observed 10 nautical miles be-
hind the Alitalia aircraft. i

“The air traffic controller sub-
mitted an occurrence report and»
investigatory action began it imme- |
diately. Extensive inquiries have :
failed to provide any indication of |
what the sighting may have been.”

A Ministry of Defence spokes-
man said yesterday: “Whatever he

“might have seen might have been’

something that was flying, but was
certainly not anything that was

- fired. It was a Sunday. The only

ranges we have in the Kent area
are Lydd and Hythe, and they are
concerned with small arms only.”
Asked if target missiles’ were |
used, he said: “It’s absolutely in
the middle of the busiest air traffic
area. People just don’t fire mis-
siles there, but of course, we do
have quite a few UFO reports and
often people who see these things
describe them as missile or cigar-
shaped, or else round, and some-
times they do appear to be travel-

ling with no’means of propulsion.”

6 May 1991.

Dear Sir/Madam,

I am writing to you with the hope that you may beable to help me. I
have seen various press reports ( Daily Telegraph, Guardian, Daily
Star ) which refer to the sighting of an object described as being

3 metres in length and cylindrical. This object was apparently ob
“served at approximately 22006 feet on April 21 around 9pm by Captain
Achille Zaghetti who was flying en route from Milan to Heathrow. The
sighting apparently occurred over Kent. My reason for writing is to
enguire if you can help on several points.

1. Has there been an official MOD investigation of this case, or has
the MOD assisted in an official Civilian Aviation Authority invest-
~igation?

2. Can you confirm that the MOD dees not have an air-to-surface missile
which could go to 22000 feet?

3. Is the Rapier missile only launched from Aberporth, Wales and Tain in
Scotland, and is its limit around 10000 feet ?

4- Could the sighting be connected with the firing range at Lydd 7

5. Is it possible to obtain copies of any records relating te this case
which may have been forwarded to the MOD? 4s I understand it a tracking
was made at West Drayton, Is it possible to obtain copies of any final
reports pertaining to this case?

Should you be able to help in any way it would be very mush appreciated

as this is just for my own interest. Thankyou for taking the time to read
my letter. I have enclosed a SAE.

Yours Faithfully,

Nicholas Redfern,

ATC OCCURRENCE REPORT - REPORT OF MISSILE TYPE OBJECT -
21 APR 91
Reference:
A. CAA 91/01151 dated 10 May 91.
1. At Reference you requested military comment on the title
Occurrence Report.
2. All the information was passed to the MOD and was examined

by the departments responsible for the air defence of the UK.
We are advised that those departments have not been able to
confirm the identity of the object sighted by the Alitalia MD80
crew.

3. The MOD were able to rule out the possibility that the
object was a missile from the Army firing ranges in the Lydd
area. Additionally, MOD has no report of any space related
activity which could provide an explanation, and the description
does not correspond with that expected if the object had been a
meteorological balloon.

4. In the absence of any clear evidence which could be used to
identify. the object, we are informed that MOD will treat this
sighting like that of any other Unidentified Flying Object and
therefore will not be able to undertake any further investigation
into the sighting. We now propose i e Report

is closed.

Sec(AS)2a

CAA REPORT OF AIR INCIDENT NEAR LYDD ON 21 APRIL 1991

Reference: D/Sec(AS)8/1 dated 21 May 1991

1. The Reference sought advice on a letter from a Mr
Nicholas Redfern about a UFO sighting over Kent on 21 April.

2. The only RN surface to air missile capable of reaching
22000£t is the Sea Dart. However, as there were no test
firings on the day concerned, I can confirm that one of
these could not have been the cause of this incident.

operations at individual DRA sites. I can, however, confirm that
the DRA has no missile firing facility which could possibly
account for a UFO over Kent.

Dear Sir, - a\3 comes 21st May 1991

I would be grateful if you could satisfy my curiosity in respect of
the statement issued to the Independent newspaper by an MoD spokesman
published on 6th May 1991, and the other matters contained herein:

a) This is in respect of the Alitalia ‘near-miss' on 21st April 1991
in civilian airspace above Kent.

Included with his statement was the following:

"People just don't fire missiles there, but of course, we do have
quite a few UFO reports and often people who see these things describe
them as missile or cigar-shaped, or else round, and sometimes they do
appear to be travelling with no means of propulsion.”

Is this not a contradiction-in-terms to the statutory response issued
by the MoD in relation to incidents of this nature over the years? Is
is it a new 'liberal' response by AS2, DI(55)B or P&SS? Or was it an
unqualified response by a spokesman from another department?

For research & reference purposes, I would be grateful if you could
clarify the position, and the name and particular department which
issued the statement in the first place,

b) An equally important matter has surfaced very recently which
requires an authorititive explanation. When sending a recent 'fax'!
message to the offices of the Daily Star newspaper, the recepient's
copy number came back in print as G-3, and not that of the Daily Star.
Can you afford any explanation why the Daily Star '‘fax' number was
changed to that of a Whitehall department body en-route? The relevant
material copy is available for inspection should you so require.

I appreciate that because of S.C.I. restrictions, you will probably
not be in a position to handle this particular matter yourself, but if
it could be channeled to the appropriate persons responsible for
Matters of this kind, it would at the very least make someone
‘uncomfortable' for allowing this extraordinary ‘gaff' to have
occurred in the first place.

if

[2]

c) Can you comment on a recent UFO incident which took place over the
North Sea, during which RAF interceptors 'chased' an object out at
sea?

Can you confirm that controllers at West Drayton observed this
incident [which was duly processed through normal channels].

Having spoken with West Drayton control staff, can you confirm that
radar plots of the intercept are a matter of record, and will the MoD
consider making these publicly available, as has the Belgium
government in respect of similar occurrences lately?

Finally, just to digress a little if I may. Can I extend an ‘open-
invitation' to you and your colleagues to attend our Leeds Conference
on Saturday 14th September at the Civic Theatre? If nothing else, you
may learn just what it is you have been protecting over the years. You
don't have to make yourselves known to us, but I hope you do a better
job of it than the C.I.A. did in Frankfurt, and the South African's in
Munich....

Assuring you of our best attention at all times, I am,

Yours faithfull

Sec(AS)2a

CAA REPORT OF AIR INCIDENT NEAR LYDD ON 21 APRIL 1991

Reference: D/Sec(AS)12/2 dated 9 May 1991

1. In response to Mr N Redfern’s letter dated 6 May 1991 I
would suggest the following responses to his questions 2, 3 and
4, which relate to ranges and missile capabilities:

Question 2 (I assume Mr Redfern means surface to air missiles)

The only surface to air missiles that could go as high as 22,000
feet are the Bloodhound (operated by the RAF) and the Sea Dart
(operated by the Royal Navy).

Question 3

The Rapier missile is launched from the range at Benbecula in the
Western Isles and the Aberporth range in Wales. Precise details
of the operational ceiling of Rapier missiles is classified, but
they could not go as high as 22,000 feet.

Question 4

The sighting in question would not be connected with the range at
Lydd, which only has anti-tank, light mortar, grenade and rifle
ranges, as well as limited field firing facilities.

2. I have attached the information that I received, in case
you need additional details.

N G Pope

Mr Tom King M.P.,

The Secretary of State for Defence,
Ministry of Defence,

Whitehall,

London.

Dear Mr King,

I refer to an incident reported in ’The Sunday Times’ dated 5th
May 1991, in which it was alledged that an unidentified ’missile’
almost collided with an Alitalia passenger airliner en route from
Milan to London. It was also reported that the near miss occurred
almost directly above a Ministry of Defence firing range. I
understand, however, that the Ministry of Defence has stated that
the object had not originated from any British armed forces. The
corollary of this statement is that some (assumedly unauthorised)
agency is launching projectiles in British airspace, thereby
creating a very serious threat to civilian air traffic.

I have conducted some personal research into reports of such
unidentified incursions into our airspace and it appears that
this is by no means an isolated incident.

I should like to know the findings of investigations into this
matter carried out by the Ministry of Defence, and what actions
Her Majesty’s Government intend to take to ensure the safety of
aircraft. I urge you to authorise a public enquiry into this and
the many similar cases of such intrusions into British airspace.

A copy of this letter has been sent to my member of Parliament,
Mr Paul Murphy M.P.

MINISTRY OF DEFENCE

Mr N Redfern

Your reference

Our reference D/Sec(AS)12/3

a . Date Q sguly 1991

Jour Hr Rae

Thank you for your letter of 6 May 1991 regarding the sighting of
an object over Kent on 21 April.

I can confirm that MoD has been asked to assist with the Civil
Aviation Authority examination of the Air Incident report made by
the Alitalia MD80 aircraft’s pilot.

The only surface-to-air missiles that could go as high as 22,000
feet are the Bloodhound (operated by the RAF) and the Sea Dart
{operated by the Royal Navy). The Rapier missile is launched
from the range at Benbecula in the Western Isles and the
Aberporth range in Wales. Precise details of the operational
ceiling of Rapier missiles is classified, but they could not go
as high as 22,000 feet. The sighting in question would not be
connected with the range at Lydd, as this has only anti-tank,
light mortar, grenade and rifle ranges as well as limited field
firing facilities.

It will not be possible for MoD to send you any of the
correspondence relating to the Alitalia pilot’s Air Incident
report. However, you may wish to contact the Civil Aviation
Authority separately.

MINISTRY OF DEFENCE 4

Hillingdon House Our reference D/Sec(AS)12/3

Middlesex UB10 ORZ Date 2 July 1991

Door Sipodion Leod
Thank you for your letter of 21 May 1991 regarding the report of

an air incident on Sunday 21 April 1991. I am sorry it has taken
me so long to respond.

I can confirm that the Alitalia MD80 captain’s report and the
LATCC controllers’ report have been examined by those
departments within MoD responsible for the air defence of the UK.
These departments have not been able to confirm the identity of
the object sighted by the Alitalia MD80 crew.

However, we have been able to rule out any possibility that what
was seen was a missile from Army firing ranges in the Lydd area.
Additionally, we have no reports of any space related activity
which could be used to provide an explanation for what was seen,
and the description of the object given by the Aliitalia captain
does nat correspond with what would be expected if the object had
been a meteorological balloon. .

In the absence of any clear evidence which could be used to
identify the object it is our intention to treat this sighting

like that of any other Unidentified Flying Object and therefore
we will not be undertaking any further investigation into the

sighting.

From: GE secretariat (Air Staff£)2a, Room 8245.

I hope that this is helpful.

CAA REPORT OF AIR INCIDENT NEAR LYDD ON 21 APRIL 1991

Reference: D/Sec(AS)8/1 dated 21 May 1991

1. The Reference sought advice on a letter from a Mr
Nicholas Redfern about a UFO sighting over Kent on 21 April.

2. The only RN surface to air missile capable of reaching
22000ft is the Sea Dart. However, as there were no test
firings on the day concerned, I can confirm that one of
these could not have been the cause of this incident.

AUTHORISATION FOR THE RELEASE OF INFORMATION

‘Applicant:

Case Number: 04-07-2005-091842-004 Expiry: 27/07/2005

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

HE would be most grateful if we could send him information now

released for the RAF Bentwaters/Rendlesham Forest alleged UFO incident of

December 1980. Also, any other declassified information — especially of RAF
aircraft sent to pursue such phenomena.

Case for release of information

We have tol nat he can look up all the information on the
Rendlesham Forest incident at The National Archives and on the MOD web.
We informed him that we do not scramble RAF aircraft to pursue such
phenomena. Precise occasions when this may have occurred has been
withheld under Section 26 of the Freedom of Information Act 2000.

Authorisation

| hereby give authorisation for the release of the aforementioned information
to the Applicant.

Date:......... .) ix feoes betes eeereteeeeeeeen
Cea

- Lam writing concerning your request for information about the RAF Bentwaters/Rendiesham
Forest incident. Your request has been passed to this Department as we are the focal point
within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence regarding UFOs.

All the documents the Ministry of Defence holds on the events at Rendlesham Forest have
already been released to the public and can be seen on the MOD Freedom of Information
Publication Scheme at www.foi.mod.uk. A search under “Rendlesham Forest” will take you
directly to these documents. Alternatively if you wish to see all the classes of information on
UFOs in the Scheme, please search on “UFO”. There is also further information available at
The National Archives and details can be found on their website www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

You also requested any other UFO declassified information about RAF aircraft sent to pursue
such phenomena. | should inform you that military aircraft are not scrambled to intercept
UFOs or in response to UFO sightings reported to us by members of the public. The RAF has
an established and well practised air defence capability which is ready to react to any threat
to the UK and aircraft may be scrambled if air defence experts think it is necessary. Precise
details of occasions when this may have occurred is withheld under Section 26 of the
Freedom of Information Act, as the information is likely to prejudice the defence of the British
Isles. :

If you are dissatisfied with our decision to refuse information or you wish to complain about
any aspect of the handling of this request, then you should contact the undersigned in the first
instance. Should you remain dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal review by
contacting the Director of Information Exploitation, 6" Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall,
SW1A 2HB.

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information
Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of
the Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website,
htto://informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely

Ministry of Defence

Director of Air Staff - Freedom of Information
5" Floor, Zone H

Main Building

Whitehall

London

SW1A 2HB

E.mail — das-ufo-office @ mod.uk

AUTHORISATION FOR THE RELEASE OF INFORMATION

Applicant a

Case Number: @4-07-2006-171800-04: +-- Expiry: 01/08/2005

fad FADES coy.

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

HB antec to know how many sightings there had been in the

counties of Cleveland, Durham and North Yorkshire since 1959.
Also could we tell him of reported sightings in the county of Cleveland in the
last five years including dates.

Case for release of information

| only found 3 reports for North Yorkshire for which | checked the database.
There were no reports for the other areas requested and specifically no
reports from Cleveland in the last five years. Informed him that the records
from 1959 would exceed the £600 limit set by the FOI Act. Hence, we could
not look that far back.

Authorisation

| hereby give authorisation for the release of the aforementioned information
to the Applicant.
Dear

| am writing concerning your request for information of the number of UFO sightings that have
been seen in the counties of Cleveland, Durham and North Yorkshire since 1959. Your
request has been passed to this Department as we are the focal point within the Ministry of
Defence for correspondence regarding UFOs.

With regard to the records we hold of UFO sightings reported to the MOD | should inform you
that they are not held electronically but are filed on paper files in the order in which they are
received. We currently hold such records spanning a 25 year period and they are not
segregated according to area or region. If we were to examine all of these records in order to
identify those from the three areas that you have requested, the cost would exceed the £600
limit set for the Freedom of information Act 2000. However, we are in the process of
compiling a database of UFO reports and this has now been completed for the years 2002,
2003, 2004 and to date in 2005. During this period there were no UFO sightings reported in
the Cleveland area or in Durham. There were 3 reports from North Yorkshire in 2004.

You may wish to be aware that there is some information about UFOs available for public
viewing. MOD files were routinely destroyed after 5 years until 1967 when they were generally
preserved for The National Archives. A few have survived before 1967 and these together
with records up to 1977 are now available for viewing. The National Archives can be
contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Kew, Richmond, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone, 020 8876 3444.
The National Archives also have a website giving information about the records they hold and
how to access them. This can be found on the internet at http:/Avww.nationalarchives.gov.uk.
The Ministry of Defence Freedom of Information Publication Scheme also contains
documents relating to the well known events in Rendlesham Forest, Suffolk in 1980 and the
final report of the Flying Saucer Working Party in 1951. These can be accessed via the
internet at http://www.foi.mod.uk. A search in the Scheme under ‘UFO’ will take you to this
information.

| hope this is helpful.

Yours sincerely

Ministry of Defence
